The dispatcher starts every task it runs as `run-task --project <dir> --task <id>`, one OS process per task run (the lab's session ends every container of its process and `ops._key_in_environment` writes the environment, so two runs in one process would end each other). `run-task` takes the task's lock, ends what dead processes left, admits and claims the task under the claim lock, and runs it; it exits 0 when the task ran or was held by a word of the held tasks, 4 when it was refused and not run (a task that is no longer `ready`, waiting, a document not taken, a cap), 1 or 3 on an error. Both run a task as follows:

**Who may run together** (`runtime/parallel.py`, `admit`, one pure function, always called under the claim lock). A ready task starts only when all of these hold: its agent has a free slot (an area agent has `max_parallel` slots, an integer from 1 to `autonomy.MAX_PARALLEL_CEILING`, default 1; skipped for a task that already holds a slot and for a project with no area agents); the project has a free slot (`max_running` in `runtime.json`, 1 to 14, default 3: the most task runs of the project at one time; the router's run counts in neither); no code task of its request is running, and no effect of its request is executing, for a code task (a request's change set keeps the newest set only, and an effect reads it); and its footprint overlaps no running task's. **The footprint** is the `outputs` and `updates` its skill declares, without the state file (`docs/workbench/state.md`, merged line by line already); a path with a placeholder (`<task>`, `<feature>`) is read as a wildcard, so it overlaps every path of its pattern, compared segment by segment. That is a limit the maintainer chose (2026-10-12): the nine skills that write `docs/engineering/plans/<task>.md`, the marketing calendar, a design `<artifact>` and a brief always overlap, so inside one floor most work still runs one task after another in this release, and different floors run in parallel; resolving a placeholder per task is backlog item R17. The existing rules stay: no open wait, an enabled agent, the daily caps (`autonomy.may_start`, which `run-next` skips as it always did).

**Who holds a slot.** A task of a request holds its agent's slot while it is `running` or `waiting` (it waits for the person), or `ready` because the person answered a decision its latest run opened (it is about to run again). A task that is `done`, `cancelled`, `failed`, `blocked`, retried after a failure (it queues like a new task) or that never ran holds none. A step task (below) holds a slot of the runtime's pseudo-agent only while it is `running`: a step task waiting for the person holds none. A task that holds a slot is never refused for slots. One edge: an answered task that a derived wait sends back to `planned` leaves the holders meanwhile; when it is ready again it is a holder at once, so a floor may briefly hold one more task than `max_parallel` (the alternative can deadlock a floor). The store reads it as `task_slot_holders`; `status` gives each task `holds_slot`, and `agents` gives each agent `max_parallel`, `slots_held`, `holders` and `max_parallel_ceiling`.

**A step task** (package P5) is a task of a request that runs no skill and calls no model: code runs it, as the step engine of `runtime/steps.py`, in a process of its own, `run-step --project <dir> --task <id>`, which the dispatcher starts for every ready step task and which `run-task` and `run-next` hand a step task to. It is admitted by the same `admit`, under the claim lock, with the same task lock: its agent is the runtime's pseudo-agent `runtime` (not an entry of `area_agents`, a name that cannot be one), which has `RUNTIME_PARALLEL` (2) slots of its own and no mode, daily cap or credential; it is never held for lack of an agent. The files it may write are its footprint, a column of the task derived from its kind, and `admit` holds it against a skill task that writes the same path, and the reverse. It is never a code task. The dispatcher starts it only if its kind says it may run unattended, and not while the person has stopped the runtime (`set-mode --agent runtime --mode stopped`, a brake kept in the store; a stop is never withheld from the page, a resume from the page needs the group `set-mode`); the person's `run-next` and `run-step` need neither. The dispatcher starts the process as `run-step --unattended`, which asks both again under the claim lock before it claims. A stop of a step task passes only on the verdict `continue`, from any channel, so a review is harmless to pass: no step performs an external effect itself (limit L30). A kind's last step may be a **gate** (package P8): it has no executor, opens an `effect` decision whose hash is the hash of the exact content, and the task waits for `approve --sha256` from the terminal or the page; code then executes the effect (`runtime/ops_step_effects.py`) and the task is `done`. An effect answered and not approved (the target changed) writes nothing and sends the task back to the gate's `redo` step (`docs/architecture/platform/outputs.md`). A step task that is killed is detected by its lock and ends `failed`, not `running`; `stop-runs --all` ends it. The kinds with a gate today are `write-back`, `output-page` and `output-table` (the effect word `write`, limit L31) `send-email` (the effect word `send`, package EMAIL-SEND, limit L42) `publish-post` (the effect word `publish`, package PUBLISH-EFFECT, limit L43) and `calendar-event` (the effect word `calendar`, package W1, limits L55 and L56): the approval of every one is `runtime/ops_step_effects.py`, which names no platform and no provider and asks the kind's module for `preflight` (the project is configured for this effect now), `caller` (the call that reaches its provider) and `idempotency_key`. No limit of the table below changes but the one it adds: a step task writes nothing outside the store but what its kind declares, and the kind `ingest` writes only through `sources.import_version` (limits L21 to L27). The kind `project-script` (package P9) is not unattended: only a person starts it. The kind is `networked` (always: it cannot know at registration whether a task declares destinations), so a routine whose flow holds one is made and changed only from the terminal or the page (limit L33), and the routine's approved content holds, for each such step, the script's sha256, its destinations, the lock file's sha256 and the definition hash of the script image (`Kind.routine_slot`, `steps.routine_slots`, the `scripts` slot of runtime/routines.py), so that a change to any of them stops the routine and asks. A routine that fires finds the task held `start it by hand`: the kind is not unattended, and running a script without a person is a later decision (limits L34 to L38).

**The locks** (`fcntl.flock` files under `<data_dir>/locks/`, all in `runtime/ops_core.py`; the project's former single lock is gone). Order, everywhere: the configuration lock, then the claim lock, then the write lock; a task lock or the route lock is taken before any of them.

| Lock | Held by | Guards |
|---|---|---|
| `task-<id>.lock` | the `run-task` process of that task (for a step task, its `run-step` process), from before its claim to after its finish | liveness: a task `running` in the store whose lock can be taken has no live holder and is an orphan. The file holds, as text, `<pid> <pgid> <start time>` of that process (the start time as `ps -o lstart=` gives it, joined by `_`; an older text has two words), read by `stop-runs --all` and by a cancel or a stop (R18: the holder it names gets TERM, then KILL after `run_stop.STOP_GRACE`, 30 s, but only when the process of that pid has that start time and a command line that is a `cli.py run-task`, `run-step` or `run-next` of this project, checked again before KILL; otherwise nothing is signalled and the answer says why, because the recovery holds the lock without writing its text and a pid is reused; a lock nobody holds is never signalled). Recovery and probes take locks without writing. The file of a task that ended `done` or `cancelled` is deleted by the next `_recover`, under its own lock (a held one stays; `_try_lock` locks again a file deleted between its open and its lock); a task that can run again keeps its file |
| `effect-<request id>.lock` | the approval of an effect (`_approve_effect`), from before its checks to the end of its execution (waits about a second) | an effect executes alone per request, and a code task of that request is not claimed meanwhile: `admit` weighs the task against an executing effect as against a running code task of its request (it fills no slot of the project or of the floor). The file is deleted, like a task's, when its request is `done` or `cancelled` and nothing holds it; probes take it shared, so they never block each other, and the approval re-reads its decision under the lock and refuses one that is no longer open. A cancel or a stop takes this lock for the whole of its call (limit L41). The approval takes the claim lock, with this lock already held, to see that no code task of the request is `running`; a claim made before is seen there, one made after is seen by `admit` |
| `route.lock` | a run of the router, for its whole run (taking it retries for about a second) | one router run per project, and its liveness |
| `claim.lock` | short (waits up to 30 s) | admission, the claim and the run row of a task run, the budget and the run row of the router, so that two processes cannot pass the same cap |
| `write.lock` | every writer of the project's files or of the state file and every external effect (`sync`, `poll`'s writes, `hand_over`, `answer`, the approval rows, the standing rows, the go-ahead's line, the Checkpoints line, `_approve_effect`, `execute_under_policy`); re-entrant per thread | one writer at a time. A run's return into the project waits for it without a timeout, because every holder is bounded (each provider call has its own timeout: the board 120 s, the documents 180 s, an effect's provider call 600 s, an engagement gate 300 s); every other writer waits up to 60 s and is then refused |
| `dispatch.lock` | a dispatch round, taken without waiting (a waiting round holds it until its children end) | one round of a project at a time |
| `upkeep.lock` | the upkeep pass (retention and the orphan containers), taken without waiting | one pass at a time; `<data_dir>/upkeep.json` holds the time of the last one, so that it runs once an hour at most (`runtime/upkeep.py`) |

**Recovery.** At the start of every dispatch round, of `run-next`, of `run-task`, of `run-step` and of a router run, `_recover` takes the lock of every task `running` in the store; the ones it can take are orphans (their process died) and are failed with the note "the run was interrupted before it ended; retry the task", with their `running` run rows; a router run row `running` is an orphan when `route.lock` can be taken (or when the caller holds it). A task whose lock is held is alive and is never failed. A run ends only through its own process or through recovery.

**The run, in order:** `run-task` reads the task, its skill and the routing before any lock that is short (the secret store is read there too, never inside the claim lock), then under the claim lock checks the documents platform, the daily cap (unless it is the person's start), `admit`, claims the task (`task_claim`) and opens the run row; the run itself holds no project lock; its return into the project's files, and the push of the documents it returned, are made under the write lock. The documents platform is read once per round in the dispatcher's process and once in `run-next`, never in a child.

The run, as before:

- **What enters the copy** (`runtime/workcopy.py`, limits L1 to L6): for a skill that requires the web, only the artifacts it declares (its `inputs`, `outputs` and `updates`) found under `docs/` and `.workbench-local/`, and the files the person handed to that task (`drop.WEB_TASK_TAKES_DROP`, the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-09: the hand-over answers `web: true` with the line "this file will be visible to a run with the open network", and `task` carries `drop: {web, takes, line}` so that a page shows the line before the send); for any other skill, the files the project's git tracks, the documents and the state file under `docs/`, and the machine files the skill declares. Never the runtime's configuration, the store or the runtime's data folder, a link or a file whose real path leaves the project, a path with a part that carries a tool's settings, work data the skill does not declare, a credential file by its name, a file too large to scan, or a file whose text looks like it holds a credential. The project's `AGENTS.md` enters only when the skill declares it, without the two lines of the workbench section the container cannot serve (recording a use and the skill check); when a glob of `protected_paths` covers it and the skill is not of a code area, only its workbench section enters. Every file left out is listed in `left_out` with its reason.
- **The text:** the request in the person's words, then "For this task:" and the task's text from the flow file; on a run made after an answer, every earlier reply of the task with the person's answer, oldest first, each answer stated as the person's decision. Right after the task's paragraph and before everything else that follows it, the language paragraph ("The language of a deliverable" below; always there, `en` when the project sets none), then the block of the person's standing instructions when the project has the file ("Standing instructions" below); no file, or a blank one, leaves the text as it was. Right before the answers, one line per document the documents mirror imported from the platform since the task's last run, when the skill lists it among its inputs and the project's file is still what was imported: "The person edited <path> on the platform since the last run; its content is theirs." Only code writes that line, from the record of imports, never from a run's text, and a run is given it once. The text names no skill: the one skill staged for the run loads by its description, as in a lab run.
- **How the skill is staged:** as in a lab run (`scripts/stage_skills.py`): the skill's folder, without its `evals/` and `scripts/tests/`, with the shared references it cites, where the adapter's tool discovers skills. Only that skill is staged. A skill that cites the platforms' folder (`shared/references/platforms/`) is also given the reference of every platform, with its data file, as a lab case is given the platforms it names: the runtime does not know in advance which platform a run will be told. They are read-only inputs: like the skill, they never come back (the path rule's `staged`).
- **Where it runs:** in the eval container of `evals/executor.py`, on the image the lab evidence is bound to, through the adapter's `run-prompt.sh`, on the model chosen for it (`runtime/ops_models.py`: the person's `--model` for this run, the task's, the request's, the agent's fixed model, the project's `default_model`, else `auto`, the cheapest `reliable` pair with a key, a price and a measured usage); it is released without review only where the skill is `reliable` on that model by the lab's evidence or the person's own tests, the proof holds on this checkout and machine and a key for the model is found. A copy that carries a tool's settings is refused before any model call.
- **The network:** a skill that requires `search:web` runs on the open network and may search and fetch pages; any other run reaches only the model provider, through the egress proxy. The one run of such a skill that code starts for a line is the web research ("A question answered by a web search"): it runs on the open network with no file of the project in its copy.
- **The credential:** never enters the container. The model's credential is read from the secret store on the machine and held by a key proxy outside the run container, which adds it to each call. Every value passed to a run is replaced by its marker in everything the run leaves, before anything is read or stored.
- **Limits of time:** the gate file's `timeout_seconds` (1,800) per attempt, and its retries for a timeout, a failure of the adapter, a refusal or an early end. A refused credential is never retried. On the account limit the run pauses and starts again afterwards, without counting as a failure.
- **What comes back:** each file the run created or changed is given one class by the path rule. `document` (Markdown under `docs/`), `machine` (any other file under `docs/`, and `.workbench-local/`) and `state` come back; `state` through the merge of `runtime/state_merge.py`, line by line on top of what the project has now (L10), so a state file that changed at the origin during the run is merged, not kept whole. A document bound to an approval, and a machine file a skill's runtime manifest names, come back as `machine` (`runtime/workcopy.py`, `returning`). Only a regular file with its real path inside the copy comes back, a file that changed in the project while the run was in progress is never overwritten, and a file whose text looks like it holds a credential, or that is too large to scan, stays in the run folder. The reply the person reads in a pending decision has every line that looks like it holds a credential replaced by a marker (`body_masked` in the payload counts them); the run folder's `response.md` is left as it is. What does not come back is listed in `kept` with its class and reason. Nothing in the project is deleted. A versioned file comes back only from a code task (a skill of a code area), as a change set made against a commit, and such a run is refused while the project's tracked files other than the state file carry uncommitted changes; any other task is not held to that, and a versioned file outside `docs/` it changed is listed in `kept`.
- **Where it is kept:** the run folder `<data_dir>/task-runs/<run id>/`: `prompt.md`, `cwd/` (the copy as the run left it) and `outputs/` (`response.md`, `timing.json`, `raw.json`, `stderr.log`, `stream.jsonl`); an attempt made again is set aside beside them. The run's row in the store records the rest.

## The contained run

`contained-run` (`ops.contained_run`) runs one skill of an area agent's pack once, in the eval container, for a caller that is not a task: the agent run of a handler (the social agent's comment and vote runs). It is the one mode of execution of an agent: the run is the one the battery measured, so the proof of the skill stands for it, **provided the task text is the measured one**. A comment, a vote file or a page a stranger wrote reaches only a copy.

```text
python3 runtime/cli.py contained-run --project <dir> --skill <name> --prompt-file <f> --out <dir>
                                     [--platform <name>]... [--timeout-seconds <n>]
```

It checks, before anything is copied, that the configuration's hash is the accepted one, that the skill is a folder of the checkout's `skills/` with a whole runtime manifest, and that it is in the pack of an enabled area agent of the configuration (a check of scope, not of mode): a project that names no area agent puts no skill in scope, and nothing is allowed that no pack names (exit 2; a configuration that was not accepted is exit 3). The model and the adapter come from the choice (`runtime/ops_models.py`, `route`: the project's `default_model`, else `auto`, with the runtime's own keys as `run-next` passes them), never from what a handler asks or from the configuration's `harness`; a skill that is not proven on that model runs supervised. The copy holds the artifacts the skill declares and nothing else (the form of limit L3), and never the runtime's configuration, although a skill may declare it as an input. The run is one attempt, in a new folder `<data_dir>/contained-runs/<n>/`, and the platform references the skill cites are staged as in a task run, with each `--platform` added. `<out>/response.md` holds the reply, past the credential scan of limit L14 (a line that looks like a credential is replaced by a marker that names the labels, never the value); `<out>/timing.json` holds `total_tokens`, `duration_ms`, `exit_code` and `cost_usd`, which is null unless the credential of the run is metered (a run on a subscription or a free credential has no billed cost). They are the two files and the keys `run-agent.sh` writes, so a handler reads them as it did. The command prints `{"status", "failure", "model", "adapter", "ignored_changes", "run_dir"}` and exits 0 when the model answered, 1 when it did not, and 3 when no run could be made: no eval image on this machine, a configuration the lab refuses, a platform with no reference. It never builds the image.

The prompt a caller gives is the one the run receives, with one addition: when the project has a standing-instructions file ("Standing instructions" below), its block is appended after the caller's text, separated by a blank line (`ops_instructions.contained_prompt`); a file that fails a check refuses the run (exit 3) before a folder is claimed. A project with no file, or a blank one, sees no change.

Four restrictions, each held by code:

| # | Restriction | How code holds it | Test (`runtime/tests/test_contained_run.py`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | A malicious comment reaches only the copy with that agent's documents | The copy holds only the artifacts the skill declares (limit L3's list), never `runtime.json`, never a versioned file the skill does not declare, no `AGENTS.md` it does not declare | `test_a_contained_social_run_sees_only_the_artifacts_its_skill_declares` |
| R2 | The model can read, answer and run commands in the container, with no credential | The lab facade passes only what `lab.reference(model)` names; the publishing credential never leaves the handler's process | `test_no_credential_but_the_model_s_own_enters_a_contained_social_run` |
| R3 | What it can spend is bounded | One attempt (no retry), the caller's `--timeout-seconds`, never the open network (`web` is false whatever the skill requires), the model from the choice (the project's default or `auto`), never from what the caller asks, and the runtime's own capped key for a model that has one | `test_a_contained_social_run_is_one_attempt_within_its_time_limit_and_never_on_the_open_network`; `test_the_model_of_a_contained_social_run_comes_from_the_proof_never_from_the_configuration` |
| R4 | Something can leave only through the reply | Nothing comes back from the copy: no file is brought back (their number is `ignored_changes`), and the reply passes the credential scan before it is written out | `test_nothing_but_the_reply_leaves_a_contained_social_run`; `test_the_reply_passes_the_credential_scan_before_it_is_written_out` |

The core of this run (`ops_contained.routed` and `execute`) has a second caller, `ask` ("The answer from sources" below), which adds to the copy the converted text of the sources code selected; the four restrictions hold for it as they do here.

The proof is the battery's, with the same task text as the measured cases: the social handler's text is compared with the cases' prompts byte for byte (`runtime/tests/test_social_task_text.py`: `test_the_engage_task_text_is_the_prompt_of_the_measured_runtime_cases` and `test_the_vote_task_text_is_the_prompt_of_the_measured_runtime_cases`), and `test_a_contained_run_is_given_the_platform_the_cases_name` that the run is given the platform the cases name. `test_a_missing_image_is_a_refusal_and_never_a_build` holds that a missing image is a refusal; `test_the_platform_s_reference_is_staged_as_the_lab_stages_it_for_a_case` that the references are those of a lab case; `test_the_cost_of_a_run_on_the_reference_model_is_written_as_unknown` that the cost is never guessed.

## The answer from sources

Package AQ-1 (`docs/architecture/platform/sources.md` is the map). `ask` (`ops.ask`, `runtime/ops_ask.py`) answers one question from the converted text of the project's sources: one run of the **answer skill** in the contained core ("The contained run" above; `ops_contained.routed` and `ops_contained.execute` are the one core both callers use), no plan to approve, with the quotes the answer rests on and the sources and versions read. It exists because a question has no effect and writes nothing to the project: a plan per question would protect nothing, and the one approval is the person's widening of the group `ask` (below), once.

```text
python3 runtime/cli.py ask --project <dir> --text <question>      # the terminal's verb; /ask <question> on the conversation, the page and a messaging app
POST /api/v1/projects/{p}/ask   {"text": "<question>"}              # the page: a job
```

- **The skill.** `runtime/roles.json` has an optional key `answer` (`runtime/roles.py`): the skill that answers. A checkout that names none replies `NOT_BUILT` ("No skill answers from the sources in this checkout yet (asst-answer is planned).") and runs nothing. The skill must be in the pack of an enabled area agent (the check of scope of the contained run); that agent owns the run (`plan.agent_of`), so its mode, its caps and its spend count it: a run whose task is a request has no agent, and `ops_core.attributed` (one helper, used for the planning agent's check, the day's use of every agent and the costs) gives an answer run to the owner of the answer skill (`ops_ask.run_owners`), as it gives a router run to the planning agent. `autonomy.may_start` is asked under the claim lock, with the billing of the credential the run would use: "The <agent> agent may not start now: <why>. Nothing was run."
- **The sources are chosen by code** (`runtime/ask_select.py`, pure; the numbers are choices of the specification, not measured): `@<id>` mentions name exactly the sources to read (at most `ASK_SOURCES_MAX` = 5, together at most `ASK_BYTES_MAX` = 256 KiB of converted text: `TOO_MANY`, `TOO_LARGE` with each id and size); with no mention, all the project's documents that have a version when they fit (a mail or calendar snapshot is not one of the documents of those two rules: it is read only when the question names it with `@<id>`, and such a question is accepted only from the terminal, the conversation's own shell and the page (from a messaging app it is refused, unless the sender's profile lists `ask-mail` ("Natural requests"), with a fixed sentence, `SNAPSHOT_HERE`, that says to ask in the terminal or on the page, and nothing runs; the MCP mode never has `ask`); a project with only snapshots answers `NO_MATCH` and offers their ids); otherwise a ranking of the question's words (lower case, no accents, three letters or more, minus a closed list of English and Portuguese stop words) against the words of each catalog entry (name, description, headings, sheet and column names, sample values), where a word matches on equality or a shared start of five letters, the score is the number of distinct words matched, ties go to the newer version date and then the id, taken in order within both caps (a single source over the cap is skipped and named). Nothing matching: `NO_MATCH` with up to ten ids to name with `@`; no source with a version: `NO_SOURCES`; neither runs a model. A model never chooses the sources: the catalog is third-party text, and an entry cannot steer which documents are read. A question is at most `ASK_TEXT_MAX` = 2,000 characters.
- **The copies** are made as a task's are (`sources.task_copies`, through `ops_sources.copies_for`): the newest version of each id, hashed against its record first (a version changed outside the runtime is not handed over), the converted text only, written to the run's prepared folder and scanned again by `workcopy.entering` (limits L26 and L14). A version the person changed by hand (`changed-by-person`) is given only to an ask typed where the person is (the terminal, or the conversation's own shell); from the page and from a messaging app it is refused with the task's sentence, because a line from a phone is unattended in the sense of the sources. The prompt is the question, the measured `ASK_TASK_TEXT` and the task's sources line; the run is one attempt, web off, within `ASK_TIMEOUT` = 300 seconds, on the model of the choice (the project's `default_model`, else `auto`).
- **The reply is checked by code** (`runtime/ask_reply.py`, pure), and a model's text never passes for what code checked. The skill writes `Answer: found | partial | not-found`, the answer with a `[source id]` after each claim, `Quote [source id]: "<passage>"` lines and `Instructions found in external content:`. A first line outside the list: the reply is shown whole after `UNCHECKED`. A quote line (a bullet or spaces before it and any case are read) is never shown as the model wrote it: a quote that is in its source's prepared copy (Unicode NFC, white space collapsed) and is at least 20 characters AND 3 words long is printed by code as `Checked quote [id]: "..."`, a form only code writes; any other quote is shown after a sentence that says what is wrong (`QUOTE_NOT_FOUND`, `QUOTE_TOO_SHORT`, `QUOTE_TOO_LONG` over 300 characters, `UNKNOWN_SOURCE`); any other line that has `quote [` in it or starts with `checked quote` is left out and flagged `QUOTE_IMITATED`, compared after NFKC, with the Unicode format characters, the combining marks and the default-ignorable code points (the fillers, the variation selectors, the tag characters) removed, the Cyrillic, Greek, Armenian, Cherokee and Latin small-capital look-alikes of Latin letters folded and the case folded (so a Cyrillic o, a zero-width space or a full-width bracket do not pass; the opening of a checked quote is compared in EVERY language of the phrase table, whatever the project's language is, so the translated form is imitated no more than the English one); an id of the form of a source id that the run was not given is flagged `UNKNOWN_SOURCE` (`[1]` is a footnote and is not); `found` or `partial` with no verified quote is `UNVERIFIED`. `not-found` sends only the fixed "Not found in the sources read: <ids and versions>.", the instructions lines and the footer. After the instructions line the reply keeps only a line that names a source the run was given, says `not followed` and carries a quoted passage of two words or more that is in the copy of the source it names (an instruction found in a source is told and never followed); every other line there is left out and flagged (`AFTER_INSTRUCTIONS`, `INSTRUCTION_UNVERIFIED`); a line that is kept is written again by code, `[<id>] "<passage>" (not followed)`, so none of the model's other words reach the person. What is shown is cleaned (NFC, with the format characters and the default-ignorable code points removed; the other combining marks stay, since the vowel signs of Devanagari and the vowel marks of Thai are part of the words, and only the imitation check drops them), and a line that holds a text-direction control is left out and flagged (`BIDI_CONTROL`). Code appends `Read: <id> (version <n>), ...` and, when the pair of skill and model is not proven (the run's autonomy is not `proven`), `Unproven: <skill> has no passing test on <model>; check the answer against the quotes.` There is no review to hold for a reply, so unproven is a label and a record, nothing more. The reply passes the credential scan (limit L14) after the footer is added. The answer follows the language the question is written in; the sentences code adds (the refusals, the frame of a checked quote, `Read:`, `Unproven:`) are said in the project's language by the phrase table (`runtime/phrases.py`, "The language of a deliverable" below); the form the skill writes, and the one `Instructions found in external content:` heading code reads, stay English. Before the reply is returned the configuration is read again: a sender removed or narrowed meanwhile gets "The answer was withheld" (the run stays recorded).
- **Records.** The question is the `text` of a request titled "Question <n>" (no text of a question is in a title, so none is in `status`, the conversation's state answers or the board); the run is a row of `task_runs` whose task is that request (`answer_run_start`, which is `route_run_start` with no web and makes the request in the same transaction: one conversation model call per project at a time, so a router run and an answer run exclude each other, and `ask` holds the route lock for its whole run; a busy lock is `RunBusy`, before the line is stored), with the cost, tokens, autonomy and the folder `<data_dir>/contained-runs/<n>/`; the rows `task_sources` name the versions the run received; the asker is the cursor `ask-actor:<run id>`, kept after the run with the same retention as the actor of a decision (the store, outside git, never deleted), and the terminal's `task` read shows it in its public form (`asked_by`: `<profile> on <platform>`, `page`, `terminal`, never a sender's id); `answer_run_finish` ends the run and the request in one transaction (`done`; a failed run is recorded failed and cancels its request by `code:answer-failed`; a request being cancelled has the run recorded `cancelled`, and `cancel <request id>` while the run goes on ends it as it ends a router run). No column, state or trigger is new. Nothing is written into the project's files. A request `done` with no task reads as finished in `status`, `progress` and the conversation, and the task board's mirror never writes it (`board.asked`: a request with no task and a run of a skill other than the router's). A typed `/ask` leaves in the conversation the line and the reply, the reply stored with the id of its answer run and no request (so it is not the router's memory); `conversation` is told the channel that reads it and shows the reply only to the channel that asked (the terminal, the page; on a messaging app only the sender), and everything else, the MCP mode always, gets "(answered from the sources: request <n>)" in its place. A process that died during an ask leaves its run ended by the next recovery (the next ask, a task's run, the dispatcher's round), which also cancels the question it left (`answers_abandoned_cancel`: a request that code titled "Question <n>" and that has no run of the router's skill, with no task and no run running).
- **The row and the policy.** The row `ask` (argument `text`; channels the terminal, the conversation, the page and messaging, never the MCP mode, whose client would get document text it did not write; `model`, `job`, `channel_arg`) is in the group `ask` of the channel policy, which is in no default: a messaging sender gets it only from a profile or a channel entry that lists it, accepted with `--widen` (POL-2), and `GROUP_TEXT` says what it gives ("... the answer and its quotes are sent to this channel (on a messaging app they pass the platform's servers)"). The page keeps it in its ceiling unless its policy narrows it. The policy is asked again inside the operation (`ops_actor.guard` for a messaging sender, `operations.refusal` for the page and the MCP mode). On a messaging app the bridge asks the group `ask` for a typed `/ask` (not `talk`): a profile that lists `ask` and not `talk` can ask and cannot write a plain line. A typed `/ask` is a turn that runs a conversation model call: a held route lock refuses it before it is stored (`RunBusy`); on the page it is queued like a line and answered, as a page ask, by the drain. The answer never enters the router's memory (it is stored with its run's id and no request, as a command's reply is). The bridge's `HELP` lists `/ask`. When the planning agent answers a plain line with a direct turn, the reply ends with `ASK_HINT` ("To answer from your sources, send /ask <question>.") only when an answer skill is configured, the sender may ask on that channel, and the project has a source with a version.
- **Limits.** L49: an answer run receives only the converted copies of the sources code selected for the question, plus the artifacts the skill declares, and writes into none of the project, a source or the documents platform; in the store it writes only its run, its request, its `task_sources` rows, its actor cursor and the reply as a message of the conversation. L50: an answer leaves only as the reply to the shell or chat that asked, opens no effect, and is readable only by the channel that asked (the conversation shows it to that channel and gives every other reader a placeholder). Threats: a stolen phone can read documents through answers (off by default, per sender, the hand-changed version refused off the terminal, the owning agent's caps); the platform's servers see the answers and the quotes (not end to end; `notices: count` does not cover replies); a planted instruction in a source is data (the selection is code).

## Natural requests

Packages NR-1 and NR-2 (`docs/architecture/platform/messaging.md`, rules 54 and 55; the decisions of 2026-10-18 in `docs/decisions.md`). A person writes what they want in their own words, on any channel, and the platform does the operation the line means, checked by the same policy as the typed command, or answers with the exact place where that act is done. Slash commands stay as shortcuts. The reminders in words (NR-1: `candidate`, `understand`, `complete`, the time parser and the language tables) are the first half of `runtime/natural.py`; what a line may do with EVERY operation of the platform is the second half: `CLASSES` is the ONE literal that gives each operation its class, one line each (no other table says it), `NATURAL` its slots, and the closed tier-1 forms are data of the language tables (`requests`). `runtime/tests/test_natural_table.py` walks `operations.OPERATIONS` and fails when an operation has no class, so "everything" stays true as operations are added, and pins the classes, so a move between classes is a one-line change in `CLASSES` and one in that test.

| Class | Operations | What a natural line does |
|---|---|---|
| R read | `status`, `progress`, `pending`, `task`, `agents`, `skills`, `costs`, `artifacts`, `artifact`, `instructions`, `routines`, `sources`, `attachments`, `reminders`, `flows`, `flow-check`, `connections`, `commands`, `items`, `file-read`, `research` | the operation, at once, the result written as lines by code from its scalar fields (a source's list is its id, type, date and the person's description: no heading, no sample value); `status` and `progress` are the prose of `state_reply`; a field that names this machine or carries a command is never written |
| N notice or own write | `remind`, `reminder-forget`, `reminder-restore` | done by the reminders' own conversation (NR-1: the bridge, before a line reaches `say`); a notice the ROUTER chose asks first by the same rules (below), except `reminder-restore`, which the router can never bring back: the reply is its command |
| W confirmed write | `cancel`, `stop-task`, `retry`, `routine-pause`, `routine-cancel`, `routine-create`, `routine-change`, `attachment-add`, `attachment-discard` | never run from a line. Until package NR-4 (a code-made confirmation card bound to the sha256 of the exact call) the reply is the exact command to send (`/cancel 4`, `/add 3`: a slash line of the conversation or the messaging app; the terminal's command for the others, never on a messaging app, where the reply is the plain sentence and the page, limit L62) |
| D decision | `approve`, `reject`, `release`, `answer`, `go-ahead` | never decided from a line. On a messaging app the reply says the decision is made from its card and the bridge sends that card again (`result["resend"]`, `Bridge.resend`, the rules of any card); elsewhere the reply is the command |
| E effect on the person's accounts | `event-create`, `event-change`, `event-cancel`, `mail-send`, `mail-reply`, `mail-draft`, `mail-archive`, `mail-move`, `mail-mark`, `mail-trash`, `file-create`, `file-replace`, `file-save`, `file-trash` | never EXECUTED from a line. A line that is a write form of a family ("A write asked in words", package NR-5, limit L61) OPENS the card of its exact content through the operation's own function, with every slot from code or asked back, and the card is the approval; a line that only names one (the whole-line forms of NR-2, with no slot) is answered with the place and the exact operation to run |
| W confirmed write | `cancel`, `stop-task`, `retry`, `routine-pause`, `routine-cancel`, `routine-create`, `routine-change`, `attachment-add`, `attachment-discard` | on the page, the terminal and the conversation never run from a line: the reply is the exact command to send (`/cancel 4`, `/add 3`; the terminal's command for the others). **On a messaging app (slice S20, the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-22) the acts that take a brake or a retry, `cancel`, `stop-task`, `retry`, `attachment-add`, `attachment-discard`, run by their typed line (`ops_natural.hand_over`: the same policy check, the same refusals, the same effect), and `routine-pause` is called after the same policy check and `ops_actor.guard`, with the actor recorded beside the routine; `routine-cancel` is final and is not done from words (one sentence offers the pause and names the page); a decision or an act that a model or the router chose is run only when `turn_gate.number_believed` finds it in the line (one number equal to the model's, the leading verb of that operation, its own object, no negation, condition, time word or question); `routine-create` and `routine-change` stay on the page (limit L62)** |
| D decision | `approve`, `reject`, `release`, `answer`, `go-ahead` | `approve`, `answer` and `go-ahead` are never decided from a line. On a messaging app the reply to `approve` says the decision is approved from its card and the bridge sends that card again (`result["resend"]`, `Bridge.resend`, the rules of any card), after which the card is still the last message, so the word that follows decides it (S2); elsewhere the reply is the command. **`reject` and `release` (the safe direction: nothing is sent, written or spent) run on a messaging app by their typed line (slice S20): the card the sender was sent, the policy and the refusals are the typed command's own; a number that is not an open decision gets "There is no open decision #N."** |
| E effect on the person's accounts | `event-create`, `event-change`, `event-cancel`, `mail-send`, `mail-reply`, `mail-draft`, `mail-archive`, `mail-move`, `mail-mark`, `mail-trash`, `file-create`, `file-replace`, `file-save` | never EXECUTED from a line. A line that is a write form of a family ("A write asked in words", package NR-5, limit L61) OPENS the card of its exact content through the operation's own function, with every slot from code or asked back, and the card is the approval; a line that only names one (the whole-line forms of NR-2, with no slot) is answered with the place and the exact operation to run |
| G gated elsewhere | `give-consent`, `set-mode`, `set-model`, `config`, `accept-config`, `routine-approve`, `routine-resume`, `instructions-set`, `instructions-add`, the `source-*` writes, `flow-save`, `stage-file`, `sync`, `hand-over`, `dispatch`, `verdict`, and the terminal's own verbs | never run; the reply names the place (the terminal, the page, or both) and the exact command (`operations.command_line`); on a messaging app one plain sentence and the page link, never the command (limit L62) |
| work | `say`, `request`, `route`, `ask` | the planning agent as today; `ask` only through the router's outcome, below |

**The order of one operation reached from a line** (`runtime/ops_natural.py`, the one dispatcher, limit L54): (1) the operation has a class; (2) the operation's row lists the channel that carries the line, else the typed command's sentence (`error: /<op> is done in the terminal or on the page`; on a messaging app the family's plain sentence and the page, `PHONE_`, limit L62); (3) `operations.refusal(op, channel, policy, sender=sender)` has no refusal, else the same sentence the typed command gets and no call (a decision asks it for the operation and for `pending`); (4) by class, as the table says; a read is called as `_say_command` calls a typed command, `channel` `messaging` or `chat` (recorded as the carrying channel by `ops_actor.carried_by`) and the `sender` named. A line that is a tier-1 form runs no model: `say` takes no route lock for it, the page never queues it, and `chat_memory` leaves it out as it leaves commands out (and the reminders' lines, by `natural.candidate`). On a messaging app the bridge asks the group of the operation the form names (`ops_natural.needs`, which asks the sender's own drafts and `natural.needs`: a read its own group, a decision `pending`, the choice of a file `ask`, the answer to a question about a reminder `remind`, everything else `talk`; never an operation whose row does not list the channel), so a profile that may read the sources and may not talk is answered. **A sender who may not talk never reaches the router:** the layer asks `talk` again (`ops_say.may_talk`), such a sender never answers the router's open question, and a line that no form takes is refused with the typed `talk` sentence. The help (`commands`), typed or in words, is a fixed text answered for every listed sender, one who may not talk included (the bridge's own list on a messaging app, as for the typed `/help`), and a read whose row does not list the channel is answered "/<op> is done in the terminal or on the page" (`operations.not_enabled` says where an operation is done when no configuration or grant can turn it on there). The conversation's own shell (`chat`) is read as the terminal.

**Tier 1 and the router.** The forms of tier 1 (for the operations other than the reminders) are data of the language tables, `runtime/natural_lang_<tag>.py`, the key `requests`: regular expressions matched against the WHOLE line (lower case, no accent, every run of other characters one space), the table of EVERY language tried on every line, each with NO open tail (a verb, the object it acts on and a closed ending; `check_requests` applies a whitelist, `natural.CLOSED_FORM`: literal words, groups, `|`, an optional `?`, a class of listed letters and the integer `{n}`, and refuses anything that matches an unbounded run: `.`, `*`, `+`, `{n,}`, `\w`, `\S`, a range, a look-around), so that a request for work that starts with the same words ("create a document about our launch plan", "change the model of the pricing page", "archive the old drafts", "schedule the post for Friday") is never taken: it goes on to the router. A line with an `@` mention or more than 40 words is never a form. The only argument a form gives is an integer written in the line. The words of a kind of document (`aliases`, the `cues` that say "a file", the `generic` and `short_stop` words) and of a confirmation in a language no table ships (`yes`, `no`, in any script) are data of the same key. A scheduling verb without a calendar word is the reminders' own M8 rule (NR-1, `candidate`: with a time it asks "an event in the calendar, or something else?", and "something else" goes on to the router; "marca"/"marque" with a time and a verb with a calendar word are an event, answered with the place and the operation `event-create`). Any other line goes to the router as it did, and the router's reply may be a fifth outcome (`runtime/router.py`):

```text
Route: none (operation)
Operation: <name from the table>
Argument <slot>: <words copied from the person's line>      (zero or more)
When: YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM                                       (optional: a time the router normalised)
```

`router.read_route(reply, said=...)` reads it as `{"kind": "operation", "name", "args", "when"}` only for a request of one line (the person's line, `said`, is taken out of the conversation's memory by `ops_say.person_line`) and only when `natural.check_operation` believes it; anything else (two Operation lines, an unknown name, a name of the planning agent's own work, a slot the row lacks, an integer not written in the line, a word that is not a piece of the line, a text that is not a span of the normalised line in any script, a `When:` that is not a date and a time or is on a row with no time, two `When:` lines, an Operation reply that mixes a route) is `unclassified` and takes the path it always had. The router's `Sources:` and `Language:` lines are not read for an operation. `_route_run` finishes the run `done` and returns `kind: "operation"` (also for a request that waited in the queue: `_drain_route` cancels it by code and answers in the conversation); `_say_route` cancels the request by code (`code:operation`) and hands the operation to the dispatcher, which believes it against the line the request holds again. Nothing in the router's prompt asks for the outcome yet (package NR-3 teaches the router skill and appends the block of operations to its prompt), so with the router as it is the outcome does not occur; the plumbing is tested with a stand-in router.

**What the router chose is shown and confirmed by the reminders' own rules.** Tier 1 reads a time from the person's words with code that has the clock; the router's choice (a reminder in a language no table knows, with the time it normalised in `When:`; a reminder to forget) is a choice no code can check against the line, so it is never done at once. The operation `remind` is asked with the line `<the span of the person's words> <When>` as the words of a `/remind` (`remind(said=..., how="command")`), and the engine of runtime/natural.py answers as it does for any line: the reading SHOWN ("I understood: tomorrow, Mon 19/10, at 13:00: <words>. Confirm? (yes / another time)", the words only when the project's notices are `text`) and the reminder made ONLY after a yes to the full reading shown, for the moment shown (a pick among readings, a time or a day is never a yes), or the open question the engine asks when something is missing. A reminder to forget is shown with what it is ("Cancel reminder #7: ...? (yes / no)") and cancelled only on a yes. A reminder to bring back (`reminder-restore`) is never brought back from the router's choice: the reply is the exact command to send. The yes and no words of the language data (`natural.engine_word`) are the engine's own on every path, the bridge's and the operations layer's alike, so a yes in Spanish or in Japanese confirms what the router chose. The question is a **draft** in the one store of drafts, `runtime/drafts.py`: memory only, ten minutes, one per channel and sender (a new question replaces the old), never logged, never in the store of the project (limit L51 keeps a reminder's words in the `reminders` table alone; the conversation stores a neutral word for such a line and its reply). The messaging bridge's own questions about a reminder (`Reminders.drafts` is a view of the same store) and the operations layer's wait there, with the owner `remind`; the choice of a file for an `ask` waits there with the owner `ops`, bound to the project it was offered in (a choice from another project is never answered). Outside a messaging app a question left open more than ten minutes is swept by the next line that reaches the layer, and a routed forget confirmed in the conversation's own shell is allowed as in the terminal. The next line of that sender is the answer to the question or drops it (the bridge answers it with `Reminders.answer`; without the bridge, the row `draft-answer` of `ops_turn` does, by the same engine). **A reminder in a language no table ships** is recognised only after the router named it, so its line was stored as written; from the neutral reply stored after it (the question of a routed reminder or of a routed forget, which show the words to the chat that asked, store the neutral word), `conversation` shows the line as the neutral word to every reader but the person's own shells (the terminal, the page, the conversation), and the router's memory leaves it out (`ops_natural.private_ids`). The line is in the conversation's store as written, and in the cancelled request, until deleted: the residue of the limit below. **What the MCP mode could not read itself is stored neutrally:** the reply to a natural read whose row does not list the MCP mode (reminders, the files people sent) is stored as a neutral word and the real reply goes only to the channel that asked (limits L50 and L51).

**`ask` by words, and AQ-3.** The router's `Operation: ask` calls `ops_ask.ask` with **the person's whole line** as the question (never a model's rewrite, never the router's `Sources:`), and the sender needs the group `ask`; the sources are chosen by code in the operation itself. This supersedes the AQ-3 package of `SPEC-ANSWER-FROM-SOURCES` (a routing row to the answer skill). A typed `/ask` whose text is written as a reminder (`natural.candidate` says `remind`) ends its reply with one code line that says how to ask for a reminder in words; it is never rerouted.

**A file named in words** (`runtime/source_resolve.py`, pure, code only, no model). Before the sources of a question are selected, and when the question has no `@<id>`, the person's words are compared with each source's name (the catalog's name without its extension), its id and the person's own description: lower case, accents removed, whole words only, the aliases of a kind of document (data of the language tables: `cv`, `curriculo`, `resume`...) counted as one word. One strong match (a word of the name or id; for a line that names a kind of document, two words of the description) is the only source read, and the reply ends "I used: <name>."; several strong matches, or only weak ones (one word of a description, again only for a line that names a kind of document), list up to five candidates (id, name, type, date) and the question waits as a draft: the next line that is the number of one, or its id, asks it again with `@<id>` added; a line that names a KIND of document (an alias group: cv, invoice, contract, passport...) and matches no source says no source matches and that searching the person's accounts is planned; a bare word for a file ("file", "documents", "sources", "pdf", "arquivo") with no kind behind it is a general question, selected as it always was; a line that names no document is selected as it always was (`ask_select`). The catalog's headings and sample values are never compared. `@<id>` always wins; an id typed as it is written (two words or a digit) names the file and nothing is asked; a source whose name and id are one common word ("plan") matches only a line that names a document; a line that names two files in plain words, with no word that says a file, keeps the old selection and asks nothing new; the choice of a file by number or id is a line that waits in the queue when the router is busy, and the draft is kept.

**A write asked in words** (package NR-5a, `SPEC-NATURAL-WRITES`; the mail and mailbox family is package NR-5b; limit L61). A plain line that asks for a write to the person's own accounts ("marca dentista quinta às 10h", "salva esse pdf no drive", "remarca o evento 2 para sexta às 15h") OPENS the card of that exact write, through the operation's own function (`event_create`, `file_save`...), and nothing else: it never executes a write, never approves, and never uses a standing approval (W5 applies to typed requests only, decision Q11). **Recognition** (`runtime/natural_writes.py`, pure): a family is a pair of modules, `natural_writes_<family>` (the reading) and `ops_natural_<family>` (what needs the project), registered in `FAMILIES`; a write line is a CLOSED HEAD at the start of the line (a verb and the object it acts on, after polite words) followed by slots whose markers are closed lists of PHRASES in the language tables (`requests.writes`, never a regular expression, checked by `check_writes`; the table of every language is tried on every line). A text slot (a title, a content, a file name) is the person's own words, verbatim; a line is taken only when its family's required marker or slot is there ("cria um documento sobre o lancamento" has none and goes to the router), a line of more than 120 words, one with an `@` mention and one that also holds a reminder trigger outside a body are never a write form, a loose scheduling verb keeps the question of NR-1 (event or something else), and a line that repeats ("toda segunda") is answered "an event that repeats is not available yet", never as a single event. **The slots are code's**: a time is the engine of NR-1 (`natural.understand`, `how` `write`) on the person's words, pinned to the moment the slots are filled (a range "das 13h às 15h" gives the end; "dia todo" an all-day event; an end not after its start is asked); a number is written in the line or chosen from a list code made; an account is a service word of the line, the account of the reference, the one account that lists what the operation needs, `accounts.default` (Q2: terminal-only, a widening accepted with `--widen`) or the person's choice; a calendar or folder is a name `accounts` lists (folded) or the operation's default, and a name it does not list is answered with the ones it lists, never a near match; an event is `<source>#<n>` resolved by the operation with the caller's rights (a sender without `ask-mail` is told so and nothing of the calendar is named); a file to replace is `source_resolve.resolve` over the Drive and OneDrive sources; a file to save is one THIS sender sent. **What is missing, or has two readings, is asked back**, one question at a time in a fixed order (account, the reference or file, the time, the title, ...): the open question is a draft of the one store (`runtime/drafts.py`, owner `write`: memory only, ten minutes, one per channel and sender, never logged or stored) that the sender's next line answers (the number of an option or its word, the time engine for a time, the line itself for a title or a name), and that any other line drops; it replaces a reminder's question and says so. **The card**: `ops_natural_writes.open_line` is the one place that opens a write for a line, in the order of the L54 dispatcher: the operation is class E and its row lists the carrying channel; `operations.refusal` (the typed sentence) and, on a messaging app, that the sender is sent a card; the reading and the resolution; then the operation's own function with the carrying channel (the conversation's shell is the terminal's equal; the MCP mode never) and the sender, which ends in `account_start.start` and the card. A reading the engine is unsure of opens the card with one line before it that says which part to check (Q3); so does a time that had passed today, hours written as bare numbers behind a range marker ("de 13 a 15") and a number left among the words of the title that may have been an hour; a range written with bare numbers is read only in two closed shapes and is always checked (a range marker, two numbers and a joiner, "de 13 a 15"; a number, a dash and a clock, "13-15h", "1-2pm"; never longer than 12 hours, never a pair below 7 without am or pm, and never a number that follows a date cue such as "dia 12": that number is a day, and "sala 3 às 10h" keeps the 3 in the title and is checked), a date that does not exist ("31/02", a month above 12) is asked again with its own sentence and the time kept, and a line that starts like a change ("remarca") but is no change form is answered "a change is asked by number", never made a new event. A question is asked once more for a short reply that does not answer it and then given up in a sentence; a question that the answers leave open twice (a file name that fails the check) is given up the same way; a title, a subject or a name offered as an answer is never a question or a request for work. The terminal's reply carries the card's lines and the approve command with the exact hash; a messaging app gets the card at once (`resend`); the page has it in the list. **On a phone** a write line is recognised before `say` (`messaging_inbound.parse`, the intent `natural` with `op` `write`), runs in a job of its own with no model, no route lock and no place in the router's queue, and is not stored in the conversation (Q10); the reminder-or-event question and the loose verb's question, answered "event", open the event with the reading the question SHOWED and its words; the caption of a message of ONE file is read when it is a closed save form (Q7) and opens the save card once the file is held. **Private**: the line, each question and each answer are stored as a neutral word (`NW_PRIVATE_LINE`, `NW_PRIVATE_REPLY`; `ops_natural.stored_line`, `private_ids`); the decision a line opens is read by the MCP mode and by a messaging reader without `ask-mail` through `private_view` and `task_view` exactly as a typed one. **The numbered items** (Q6): the read `items` (`runtime/ops_items.py`) lists the messages and events of the newest snapshots with the numbers the references use, so that "o e-mail 3" points to something on a phone; it needs `item_refs.may_read` (a messaging sender with `ask-mail`), is never on the MCP mode and is stored as a neutral word. **The router's outcome for a write** (`ops_natural.run_routed`): the operation it names is only a hint; the line is read again by the family's own parse with that operation fixed, and the router's `When:` is never used for a write: a time the line's words do not give is asked.

**Mail and calendar for a holder of `ask-mail`** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-18, superseding the RT-1 rule for the holders of the group; the group the private lines of the cards of W1 to W4a and `item_refs.may_read` wait for). `ask-mail` is a group of `operations.GROUPS` (`ask`, kind `mail`), valid on a messaging app alone (`MESSAGING_ONLY_GROUPS`), in no default, and a gain over the accepted configuration: a file that gives it to a profile is accepted only with `accept-config --widen` (POL-2). It adds to `ask` and never grants it (`KIND_ONLY`: a profile lists both). **It is given in a listed sender's own profile (`messaging.senders.<platform>[].policy`, then `policies.<name>`) and never in a channel-wide list**, nor in a profile a channel names, and a profile that lists it is named by ONE sender (a second sender naming it is refused): `project_config` refuses each with a sentence, because the text of a mailbox or a calendar is read for the person it belongs to. What a holder reads of a card's private lines (`/pending`, a task, in words or typed) goes to that chat, and the conversation, which the MCP mode reads, keeps a neutral word for the reply (`ops_natural.keeps_private`). A messaging sender who lists it may name a mail or calendar source with `@<id>`, may have one found by words, sees the private lines of the cards of the writes to their accounts (and may approve them where their profile grants that effect's word), and those sources join the automatic selection of that sender's questions (`ask_select.select(..., snapshots=True)`); a sender without it keeps the RT-1 rule exactly, and the terminal, the conversation and the page are unchanged. `operations.holds(group, channel, policy, sender)` is true only for a group the project's own entry or the sender's profile lists. The group is asked again when the answer is ready: an answer that read a snapshot is withheld from a sender who no longer holds it. The text of the mailbox goes to a model and the answer passes the platform's servers (`GROUP_TEXT` says so).

**A mail write asked in words** (package NR-5b; `runtime/natural_writes_mail.py` reads, `runtime/ops_natural_mail.py` resolves, the data is `requests.writes.mail` of the language tables; limit L61). The mail family of the lines that open the card of a write to the person's own mailbox: an e-mail sent (`mail-send`), a reply (`mail-reply`), a draft (`mail-draft`, new or a reply), and 1 to 20 messages archived, moved to a folder, marked read or unread, or sent to the trash (`mail-archive`, `mail-move`, `mail-mark`, `mail-trash`). A form is a CLOSED HEAD at the start of the line (a polite word may come first) followed by slots with closed markers, all lists of phrases in the language data and never a regular expression: `VERB [article] [latest] NOUN [reference] { MARKER SLOT }* [BODY-MARKER BODY]`. A line is taken only when its family's required slot is there: an address written, or a name AND a body marker, for an e-mail; a reference for the others (the body of a reply and the state of a mark are asked when the line leaves them out); a folder marker for a move. So "manda um e-mail para todos os clientes sobre a promocao" (no address, no body marker), a reply to several messages, a line with an `@` that is not a plain address, a line of more than 120 words, a line with a time outside its subject and body (a scheduled send is not offered) and a write verb in the middle of a line are not taken, and go on to the router as before. **What the person wrote is never rewritten (Q1, the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-19: a body is verbatim, no drafting run):** a subject and a body are exact slices of the line (`spans` say where), cut only at their edges, with a matching pair of quotes around the whole removed; a body keeps its punctuation and may hold line ends, a subject is one line of at most 200 characters, a body at most 4,000. **An address is only as written (Q4):** a recipient written as a name is asked for its address, and no mailbox, snapshot, index or address book is searched for one; an address inside a body or a subject is never a recipient, and a reply or a reply draft carries no recipient at all (the operation takes it from the index of the message answered, limit L60, unchanged). **A message** is `<source>#<n>` from digits written in the line (a list `2, 4 e 7`, a range `1 a 3`), from a `<source>#<n>` written whole, or BY WORDS (Q5): the words after `do/da/from` are compared, as whole words and folded, with the words of each message of the newest snapshot's index (the local part of its from-address split on `. _ - +`, the labels of its domain except the last, and its subject), and a message matches only when EVERY content word is among them; a singular object (a reply, `o e-mail da Bia`) needs exactly one match and otherwise up to five are listed and asked, a plural object takes every match up to 20 and more are asked; `ultimo/latest` with no words is number 1 of the newest snapshot, which is read first (its age is checked); the words are at most three, with no verb and no gerund, and a day, a month, a weekday or a relative time among them is never matched (the line says messages are not picked by date). **The account** is the one a service word of the line names (`pelo gmail`, `via outlook`), else the account of the mailbox the message is in, else the only account the project configures, else the account the project chose as its default (when the configuration has one), else it is asked; **the mailbox** (the registered mail source) is the written one, else the only one of the account, else asked; **a folder** (after a folder marker whose tail is neither a number nor a service: `1 to 3` is a range) is a name of `accounts.<account>.mail_folders` equal when folded (a person after a bare `para`/`to` is a forward, which is not built), and a name that is not configured lists the configured ones and asks, never a near match. **Who may read:** a message is named, a snapshot is opened and a candidate is listed only after `item_refs.may_read` accepted the caller (a messaging sender needs `ask-mail`); a caller who may not gets the sentence of `item_refs` and nothing of the mailbox, not even the names of its sources, and the MCP mode never. `resolve` returns the arguments of the operation's own function (`mail_send(account, to, subject, text)`, `mail_reply(account, message, text)`, `mail_draft(account, to, subject, body | message, body)`, `mail_archive|mail_trash(account, messages)`, `mail_move(account, messages, folder)`, `mail_mark(account, messages, state)`), or a final sentence of the phrase table when the line can never be answered as asked (the caller may not read the mailbox, nothing matches, a card takes fewer messages), or the one question still open, in this order: the account, the mailbox, the message, the address, the subject, the body, the folder, the state; the account, the mailbox, the message (a singular object among up to five candidates, a plural one among the newest twenty) and the folder are options the person chooses by the number shown or the word (the foundation's generic answer), and `natural_writes_mail.answer` takes the rest: an address only as written, a subject or a body as the line itself (a question, a request or an over-long line is never an answer: it is asked once more and then given up), and the state by its closed words or the number of its option. The call itself, the draft of the question, the privacy of the stored line and the opening of the card are the foundation's (package NR-5a, `ops_natural_writes`, limit L61): the family reads and resolves, reaches no effect, no writer and no approval, and imports no operation of the layer.

**The interpreter reads the rest (slice S1; limit L63).** On a messaging app, a plain line that no list of code took goes, for a sender who holds the group `interpret`, to the natural-language interpreter (`runtime/ops_interpret.py`) before the planning agent: the order is the hybrid the maintainer confirmed on 2026-10-21 (the code that claims a pt/en line keeps it; the interpreter reads the rest, in any of the six languages of the served catalog). It sends the line to the one daily route with the typed frame `none`, parses and checks what comes back against the line, and carries the decision out through the calls that already exist: a read or a notice through the dispatcher above (a reminder is shown and confirmed with a yes before it is made), an event with a time through the calendar family with a seed (its card shows the weekday, the full date, the time and the zone), any other write through its family (pt/en lines; any other language is told to ask in Portuguese or English), a decision or a gated act with a fixed sentence, and a request for the planning agent through `_say_route` as before. The model's words are never shown; the conversation stores the line after the decision and as a neutral word. Enabling is the maintainer's dated decision in `runtime/interpreter_enable.json`, printed by `evals/eval_status.py interpreter` beside the bands ("enabled by maintainer decision 2026-10-21, below the gate"); it never moves a band.

**A file read by its name (slice S8; `runtime/ops_file_read.py`; the group `read-files`).** A line such as "show me the file X" is the class R operation `file-read` (slots `file`, a span of the line, and `service`, one word of it): the interpreter decides the operation and the name from the person's line alone and no model ever reads a file. **What may be read:** only a file that sits in a folder the project lists under `accounts.google.folders` or `accounts.microsoft.folders` of `runtime.json`, or in a subfolder of one, at most 5 levels down (S17). The name given is searched as words: every word of it (case, accents and punctuation do not matter) occurs in the file's name, the extension is optional, and a file whose whole name is the one given wins over files that only hold its words. The search is a breadth-first walk through the providers' `children` (one folder, up to 200 items), starts only from the listed folders, stops at 2,000 items listed or 10 seconds, and the reply says when it was cut or a folder could not be listed; no folder outside the listed ones is ever listed, whatever the account token could reach. **Narrowing:** an account word of the line ("do google drive", "no onedrive", "do drive") narrows the search to that account (the bare word "drive" names none; a line that names both searches both; the `service` slot may hold two or three words in a row; `interpreter._check_slots` checks them and `natural.check_operation` is unchanged), and a folder named in the line narrows it to that folder and what is under it. **The folder rule (S17b):** the folder is named as the person sees it in their Drive, with or without slashes, never through the alias the configuration gives a folder ("default"): "/openhora" and "openhora" mean a folder called openhora at any depth under any listed folder, "/openhora/x" the chain openhora then x at any depth, case and accents folded; a listed folder is also meant by its real name (read once from the provider for a Google folder, `stat`, or taken from the end of a OneDrive path; if that read fails the service's own name is shown) and still by its alias. Only folders the search reached are looked at. A folder that none of them carries does not stop the search: the file is looked for in all the listed folders and the reply adds one note (`FR_FOLDER_MISSING`: the folder was not found and all the listed folders were searched); the refusal (`FR_NO_SUCH_FOLDER`: nothing outside the listed folders is searched, nothing is listed) is only for a path that points outside: a `..` or `.` step, the root of a drive (`/`, `~`) or an item id. **Every reply names folders by their real names**, never the alias: "pasta Openhora", "Meu Drive" for a Drive's root, "Documentos" for a OneDrive path, the service's name when a name could not be read. The listing and the read go through the providers of `integration:documents` (`children` and `read`), the intake and the converter of a source and the credential scan (L14); nothing is registered: no source, no version, no copy, no catalog row, and the bytes live in a private staging folder for the length of the call. **Where the text goes:** into the reply to the chat that asked and nowhere else. The reply holds the text up to 3,500 characters and then says how many remain; several matches are listed as a numbered list (name, the folder path from the configured folder's own name, account) and none is read until the sender's next line is a bare number ("o 2", "the 2nd"): the list is kept in the process's memory for that one line (at most 10 minutes, never written, logged or sent), the file is read by code with no model, and the right to read files is asked again, and so is the grant (the configured folder the file was found under must still be one of the configuration in force, else the plain no-folder sentence and nothing is read); a found file or a list is followed by a note when the search was cut or a folder was listed in part or failed; outside a messaging app a list asks for the full name; no match names the folders searched by the names the configuration gives them, never an id or a path, and says that their subfolders were included; a text that looks like a credential is withheld; a provider's failure is a fixed sentence with none of its words. The conversation stores a neutral word for the line and for the reply (`extras["stored"]`), the interpreter keeps nothing, and nothing here logs a name, a folder or a text. **Who may ask:** a sender who holds the group `read-files` (in no default policy and never on the MCP mode, since no model client may be handed the person's files; the page and the terminal follow the row; widened with `accept-config --widen`, which prints "shows the text of the files in your configured Drive and OneDrive folders in the chat, found by their names; a messaging app's servers see it as they see any message, and no model reads it"); any other sender gets the refusal sentence of the operation and no provider is called. **What it does not do:** a question about a file ("what does clause 3 of the file X say") is not answered from the file: the answer from sources reads registered sources only and this slice registers none, so the text is shown; making the configured folders sources is a separate decision. The interpreter's enabling entry of 2026-10-21 names the catalog that carries `file-read`, and the replay gate keeps replaying the recorded answers against the catalog they were recorded with (`evals/interpreter/replay/catalogs/`).

**The file itself (slice S18; `runtime/ops_file_read.py`, `runtime/messaging_file.py`, `runtime/messaging_bridge.py`; the group `send-files`).** A sender who holds the group `send-files` beside `read-files` gets the found file as an attachment of the reply, on a messaging app whose provider declares the capability `send_files` (Telegram today; `providers/messenger/README.md`): a photo for a png or jpeg image under the photo limit, a document otherwise; the file goes with one short caption ("Here is the file <name> (<account>, folder <folder>).", pt-BR in the phrase table) and never with a preview of its text (S18b), and a file that is withheld for any reason gets its sentence and not the text. The folder is the configured folder's real name ("Meu Drive" for the Drive root) and the subfolders after it; when the name could not be read the account alone is named ("(Google Drive)"), never the service's name in place of a folder. A sender with `read-files` alone gets the text, as before. Without the group, with a provider that declares none, or off a messaging app, the reply is the text it was before, byte for byte. The group is in no default policy, is valid on a messaging app only, adds to `file-read` without granting it (`KIND_ONLY`, as `interpret` does), and is refused at `accept-config` unless the same profile or channel entry lists `read-files` ("'send-files' needs 'read-files' beside it"); accepting a file that gives it needs `--widen`, whose list prints the line "sends the found file itself to the chat, not only its text: the whole file leaves your drive for the messaging app's servers; one whose text looks like a password or a key is not sent (adds to read-files)". **In code, before a byte is sent:** the right and the folder grant are asked again at the send (a pick, "o 2", reads the configuration in force then: a profile that lost `send-files` or `read-files`, or a folder that is no longer configured, sends nothing), and the bridge asks the right once more when it hands the file over (`Bridge.file_to_send`: if the right to read files is gone the text found is not sent either, and the reply says that the right changed); the size is checked against the app's limit (a file over it gets one sentence, with the preview when it has text) and the type, read from the bytes, against the app's list (an executable, an archive, a video or an unidentified binary is not sent: it can be neither identified nor scanned); what is sent is what is scanned (limit L14; the wording of the limit already covers a file that leaves as an attachment). The text the converter makes is scanned as for a source, and so are the BYTES that go (`runtime/send_scan.py`, standard library only): the raw bytes of a text, csv, html or Markdown file (a script, a data link); every XML or text part of an Office package, hidden sheets and slides, properties and comments included, each decompressed within bounds (a cap per part, in all and on the number of parts), and the zip comment; a PDF's raw bytes, its strings read (hex, octal and UTF-16) and every FlateDecode stream inflated within the same bounds. A hit withholds the file AND its preview with one plain sentence.
